\displaystyle-\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\left({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}-\sum_{k}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}){\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right)\left({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}-\sum_{k}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}){\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right)^{\top}\right]\right\|_{F}.

Therefore, for 1st order term, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\bm{\hat{\psi}}_{i,1}({\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*})^{\top}\right\|_{F}\geq

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\left({\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right)\left({\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right)^{\top}\right]\right\|_{F}-\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))

\displaystyle-\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\left({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}-\sum_{k}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}){\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right)\left({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}-\sum_{k}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}){\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right)^{\top}\right]\right\|_{F}.

Note that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\left({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}-\sum_{k}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}){\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right)\left({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}-\sum_{k}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}){\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right)^{\top}\right]\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\sum_{k}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}})\left({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right)\cdot\sum_{k}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}})\left({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right)^{\top}\right]\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\left\|\sum_{k}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}})\left({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\left\|\bm{\widetilde{\psi}}_{\ell}({\bm{x}})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))

where recall
\bm{\widetilde{\psi}}_{\ell}({\bm{x}}):=\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})({\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*})
and use Lemma
E.7
and Lemma
E.10
in the last line. Also,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\left({\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right)\left({\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right)^{\top}\right]\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\right]\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}{\varepsilon}_{\pi}\right)+\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))\right)

These imply the bound on 1st order term

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\bm{\hat{\psi}}_{i,1}({\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*})^{\top}\right\|_{F}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\left\|\bm{\widetilde{\psi}}_{\ell}({\bm{x}})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]-\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}{\varepsilon}_{\pi}\right)+\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))\right).

Now combining the bound on 0th and 1st order term, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\left\|\bm{\widetilde{\psi}}_{\ell}({\bm{x}})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\geq

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\left(\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\left\|\bm{\widetilde{\psi}}_{\ell}({\bm{x}})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-\left(\mathcal{O}\left((D_{\max}^{2}+\frac{B^{4}}{\sqrt{d}}+n){\varepsilon}_{\pi}\right)+\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))\right)

Rearranging terms lead to

\displaystyle\quad\quad\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\left\|\bm{\widetilde{\psi}}_{\ell}({\bm{x}})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\left(\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}-\left(\mathcal{O}\left(D_{\max}^{2}+B^{4}\right){\varepsilon}_{\pi}+\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))\right)}{\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\begin{cases}1/d,\quad\text{if }\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq 1/\sqrt{d}\\
d\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left(\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}-\left(\mathcal{O}\left(D_{\max}^{2}+B^{4}\right){\varepsilon}_{\pi}+\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))\right),&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

∎

Finally, we relate loss
\mathcal{L}
to such parametric error bounds.
See
E.6

Proof.

By Lemma
E.9
we know

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})\leq\sum_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}^{*}\log\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}+\sum_{\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\pi_{i}\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim n{\varepsilon}_{\pi}+\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))+\sum_{\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\pi_{i}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}

where the last line we use Lemma
E.10
. Taking the square on both side and rearrange the terms we get the bound (noting that
\left\|\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq 0
and
\pi_{\ell}^{*}\leq 1
).
∎

E.4
Technical Lemma

This part collects few technical lemma that are used in the analysis.

Lemma E.7
.

For
i\in S_{\ell}
,
j\neq\ell
and constant
\alpha\geq 1
, if
\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq B\leq D_{\max}
and
\Delta^{2}\geq CD_{\max}B
for a large enough constant
C
, then there exists a constant
C_{1},C_{2}
(only depending on
\alpha
) such that

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i}\right\|_{\alpha,{\bm{x}}\sim j}:=\left(\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim N({\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*},{\bm{I}})}\left[\psi_{i}^{\alpha}({\bm{x}})\right]\right)^{1/\alpha}=\left(\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim N({\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*},{\bm{I}})}\left[\left(\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p({\bm{x}})}\right)^{\alpha}\right]\right)^{1/\alpha}\leq\frac{C_{1}\pi_{i}n}{\sqrt{\hat{\pi}_{j}\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}}\exp(-C_{2}\Delta^{2}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p^{*}({\bm{x}})}\right]\leq\frac{C_{1}\pi_{i}}{\sqrt{\pi_{j}^{*}\pi_{\ell}^{*}}}\exp(-C_{2}\Delta^{2}).

Proof.

We have

item 1

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i}\right\|_{\alpha,{\bm{x}}\sim j}^{\alpha}=

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim N({\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*},{\bm{I}})}\left[\psi_{i}^{\alpha}({\bm{x}})\right]\leq\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim N({\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*},{\bm{I}})}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\bm{x}}\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*};{\bm{x}})\cdot\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p({\bm{x}})}{\textnormal{d}}{\bm{x}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{a})}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{8}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\int_{\bm{x}}\phi\left(\frac{{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}+{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}{2};{\bm{x}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*};{\bm{x}})}{p({\bm{x}})}}\sqrt{\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p({\bm{x}})}}{\textnormal{d}}{\bm{x}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{b})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\exp\left(-(\Delta-B)^{2}/8\right)\left\|\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*})}{p}\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left(\frac{{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}+{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}{2};{\bm{I}}\right)}^{1/2}\left\|\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i})}{p}\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left(\frac{{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}+{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}{2};{\bm{I}}\right)}^{1/2}

where (a) we use
\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{8}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{x}}-\frac{{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}+{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}{2}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)
;
(b) we use
\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\geq\Delta-B
since
i\not\in S_{j}
.

To bound above, it suffices to show the following claim (first part):

Claim E.1
.

Given any
{\bm{\mu}}
that there exists
\ell
such that
\left\|{\bm{\mu}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq B
for all
i\in S_{\ell}
and any
{\bm{v}}
, then

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}})}{p}\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}\lesssim\frac{n}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}\exp(\mathcal{O}\left(\left\|{\bm{v}}-{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}B+B^{2}\right))+\frac{n}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}})}{p_{*}}\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}\lesssim\frac{1}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}\exp(\mathcal{O}\left(\left\|{\bm{v}}-{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}B+B^{2}\right))+\frac{1}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}.

Thus, using the above claim we have

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i}\right\|_{\alpha,{\bm{x}}\sim j}^{\alpha}\lesssim\pi_{i}\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))\cdot\sqrt{\frac{n}{\hat{\pi}_{j}}\exp(\mathcal{O}\left(D_{\max}B\right))}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{n}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}\exp(\mathcal{O}\left(D_{\max}B\right))}\lesssim\frac{\pi_{i}n}{\sqrt{\hat{\pi}_{j}\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}}\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2})).

item 2

The proof is similar as item 1. We have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p^{*}({\bm{x}})}\right]=

\displaystyle\int_{\bm{x}}\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*};{\bm{x}})\cdot\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p_{*}({\bm{x}})}{\textnormal{d}}{\bm{x}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{a})}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{8}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\int_{\bm{x}}\phi\left(\frac{{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}+{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}{2};{\bm{x}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*};{\bm{x}})}{p_{*}({\bm{x}})}}\sqrt{\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p_{*}({\bm{x}})}}{\textnormal{d}}{\bm{x}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{b})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\exp\left(-(\Delta-B)^{2}/8\right)\sqrt{\frac{1}{\pi_{j}^{*}}}\left\|\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i})}{p_{*}}\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left(\frac{{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}+{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}{2};{\bm{I}}\right)}^{1/2}

where (a) we use
\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{8}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{x}}-\frac{{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}+{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}{2}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)
;
(b) we use
\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\geq\Delta-B
since
i\not\in S_{j}
.

Then, using the second part of the claim we get the desired bound.

Proof of the Claim.

We have

item 1

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}\phi({\bm{\mu}})}{p}\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\pi_{i}(\phi({\bm{\mu}})-\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i}))}{p}\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}+\left\|\frac{\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i})}{p}\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\left\|\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i})}{p}\left(\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}})}{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i})}-1\right)\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}+n

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\left\|\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}})}{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i})}-1\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}+n

To bound above, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}})}{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i})}-1\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}=

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}\left[\left|\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{;}{\bm{x}})}{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}-1\right|\right]=\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}\left[\left|e^{\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\langle{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}},{\bm{\mu}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\rangle}-1\right|\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}\left[\left|e^{\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\langle{\bm{v}}-{\bm{\mu}},{\bm{\mu}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\rangle+\langle{\bm{x}}-{\bm{v}},{\bm{\mu}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\rangle}-1\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp(\mathcal{O}\left(\left\|{\bm{v}}-{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}B+B^{2}\right)),

where we use Lemma
D.17
and
\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}\leq B
.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}})}{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i})}-1\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}\lesssim n\exp(\mathcal{O}\left(\left\|{\bm{v}}-{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}B+B^{2}\right))+n

item 2

We have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}\phi({\bm{\mu}})}{p_{*}}\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}(\phi({\bm{\mu}})-\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}))}{p_{*}}\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}+\left\|\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*})}{p_{*}}\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}})}{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*})}-1\right\|_{1,{\bm{x}}\sim N\left({\bm{v}};{\bm{I}}\right)}+1

Then follow the same calculation as in item 1, we get the desired bound. We omit for simplicity.
∎
∎

Lemma E.8
.

If
\hat{\pi}_{\ell}\gtrsim\pi_{\ell}^{*}
,
\pi_{i}
is near optimal in the sense that
\pi_{i}|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}+1|\leq{\varepsilon}_{\pi}
and
\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq B\leq D_{\max}
and
\Delta^{2}\geq CD_{\max}B
for a large enough constant
C

then there exists a constant
C_{1}
such that

\displaystyle\left|\pi_{\ell}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})]-\pi_{i}\right|\leq{\varepsilon}_{\pi}+C_{1}\pi_{i}\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2})).

Proof.

Since
\pi_{i}
is near optimal, we know

\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim p_{*}}[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})]=-\pi_{i}\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}=\pi_{i}\pm{\varepsilon}_{\pi}.

Then, by Lemma
E.7
we know

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim p_{*}}[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})]-\pi_{\ell}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})]\right|=\sum_{j\neq\ell}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})]\lesssim\sum_{j\neq\ell}\frac{\pi_{j}^{*}}{\sqrt{\hat{\pi}_{j}\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}}\pi_{i}n\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2})).

This leads to the desired bound with
\hat{\pi}_{\ell}\gtrsim\pi_{\ell}^{*}
.
∎

Lemma E.9
.

Recall
\hat{\pi}_{\ell}=\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\pi_{i}
. If for
i\in S_{\ell}
,
\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq B\leq D_{\max}
and
\Delta^{2}\geq CD_{\max}B
for a large enough constant
C
, then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}^{*}\log\left(\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}+\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))}\right)\leq\mathcal{L}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})=D_{\text{KL}}(p_{*}||p)\leq\sum_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}^{*}\log\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}+\sum_{\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\pi_{i}\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Proof.

For upper bound, we have

\displaystyle D_{\text{KL}}(p_{*}||p)=

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim p^{*}}\left[-\log\left(\sum_{\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p^{*}({\bm{x}})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim p^{*}}\left[-\log\left(\sum_{\ell}\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*};{\bm{x}})}{p^{*}({\bm{x}})}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*};{\bm{x}})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{a})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{x\sim p^{*}}\left[-\sum_{\ell}\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*},{\bm{x}})}{p^{*}(x)}\log\left(\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i},{\bm{x}})}{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*},{\bm{x}})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{x\sim\ell}\left[-\log\left(\frac{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}\frac{\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i},{\bm{x}})}{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*},{\bm{x}})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{b})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}^{*}\log\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}+\sum_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{x\sim\ell}\left[-\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}\log\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i},{\bm{x}})}{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*},{\bm{x}})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}^{*}\log\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}+\sum_{\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\pi_{i}\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}}\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

where (a)(b) we use Jensen’s inequality.

For the lower bound, we have

\displaystyle D_{\text{KL}}(p_{*}||p)=

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim p^{*}}\left[-\log\left(\sum_{\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p^{*}({\bm{x}})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{a})}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{j}\pi_{j}^{*}\log\left(\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\sum_{\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p^{*}({\bm{x}})}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{b})}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{j}\pi_{j}^{*}\log\left(\frac{\hat{\pi}_{j}+\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))}{\pi_{j}^{*}}\right)

where (a) we use Jensen’s inequality;
(b) we use
-\log u
is decreasing and

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\sum_{\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p^{*}({\bm{x}})}\right]\leq\frac{\hat{\pi}_{j}}{\pi_{j}^{*}}+\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\sum_{\ell\neq j}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p^{*}({\bm{x}})}\right]\lesssim\frac{\hat{\pi}_{j}}{\pi_{j}^{*}}+\frac{1}{\pi_{j}^{*}}\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2})).

due to Lemma
E.7
that
C
is a constant.
∎

Lemma E.10
.

If loss
\mathcal{L}\leq\mathcal{L}^{(0)}\lesssim d
, for
i\in S_{\ell}

\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq B\leq D_{\max}
and
\Delta^{2}\geq C\max\{D_{\max}B,d/\pi_{\min}^{*}\}
for a large enough constant
C
and
\bm{\pi}
is near optimal in the sense that

\pi_{i}\left|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}+1\right|=\left|\mathbf{E}_{\bm{x}}\left[\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p({\bm{x}})}\right]-\pi_{i}\right|\leq{\varepsilon}_{\pi},

then
|\hat{\pi}_{\ell}-\pi_{\ell}^{*}|\lesssim n{\varepsilon}_{\pi}+\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))
.

Proof.

We start by giving a loose bound on
\hat{\pi}_{\ell}\gtrsim e^{-\Theta(d/\pi_{\ell}^{*})}\pi_{\ell}^{*}
.
By Lemma
E.9
we have

\displaystyle d\gtrsim\mathcal{L}\geq\sum_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}^{*}\log\left(\frac{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{\hat{\pi}_{\ell}+\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))}\right).

The result follows by noticing that
\Delta\gtrsim\sqrt{d/\pi_{\min}^{*}}
with large enough hidden constant.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\pi}_{\ell}=\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\pi_{i}=\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\pi_{i}\left(\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim p_{*}}\left[\frac{\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p({\bm{x}})}\right]-1+1\right)\pm n{\varepsilon}_{\pi}=\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim p_{*}}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\right]\pm n{\varepsilon}_{\pi}.

By Lemma
E.7
we know (recall
i\in S_{\ell}
)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim p_{*}}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\right]=

\displaystyle\pi_{\ell}^{*}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\right]+\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\sum_{j\neq\ell}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{\ell}^{*}-\pi_{\ell}^{*}\sum_{i\not\in S_{\ell}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim\ell}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\right]+\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\sum_{j\neq\ell}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\right]=\pi_{\ell}^{*}\pm\frac{n}{\pi_{\min}^{*}}\exp(\Theta(d/\pi_{\min}^{*})-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))

where we use the loose bound obtain at the beginning that
\hat{\pi}_{\ell}\gtrsim e^{-\Theta(d/\pi_{\min}^{*})}\pi_{\ell}^{*}
.

Together above and recall
\Delta\geq C\sqrt{d/\pi_{\min}^{*}}
with large enough constant, we get the desired bound.
∎

Appendix F
Regularity condition

In this section, we show the local smoothness of loss
\mathcal{L}
.
The proof is overall the same as in
[
42
]
for 1-GMM case.

Theorem F.1
.

At any two points
{\bm{\mu}}=({\bm{\mu}}_{1}^{\top},\ldots,{\bm{\mu}}_{n}^{\top})^{\top}
and
{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}}=(({\bm{\mu}}_{1}+{\bm{\delta}}_{1})^{\top},\ldots,({\bm{\mu}}_{n}+{\bm{\delta}}_{n})^{\top})^{\top}
, if

\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{\max\{6d,4D_{\max}+2B\}},\quad\forall i\in[n],

then the loss function
\mathcal{L}
satisfies the following smoothness property: for any
i\in[n]
there is a constant
C
such that we have

\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}+{\bm{\delta}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}},\bm{\pi})-\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}},\bm{\pi})\right\|\leq Cn(\sqrt{d}+D_{\max}+B)(D_{\max}+B)\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|+\sum_{k\in[n]}\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{k}\right\|.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|)\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{x}}-({\bm{\mu}}_{i}+{\bm{\delta}}_{i})\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)}{\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\langle{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i},{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\rangle-\frac{\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|)\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right).

Therefore,
\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})
can be bounded as

\displaystyle\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi_{i}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{x}}-({\bm{\mu}}_{i}+{\bm{\delta}}_{i})\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)}{\sum_{k\in[n]}\pi_{k}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{x}}-({\bm{\mu}}_{k}+{\bm{\delta}}_{k})\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pi_{i}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)\exp\left(\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|)\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)}{\sum_{k\in[n]}\pi_{k}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)\exp\left(-\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{k}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right\|)\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{k}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(2\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|)\right)\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi_{i}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{x}}-({\bm{\mu}}_{i}+{\bm{\delta}}_{i})\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)}{\sum_{k\in[n]}\pi_{k}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{x}}-({\bm{\mu}}_{k}+{\bm{\delta}}_{k})\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\pi_{i}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)\exp\left(-\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|)\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)}{\sum_{k\in[n]}\pi_{k}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)\exp\left(\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{k}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\right\|)\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{k}\right\|^{2}}{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(-2\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|)\right)\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}).

Recall gradient is given by Lemma
A.2

\displaystyle\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}},\bm{\pi})=\sum_{j\in[m]}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})({\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{x}})\right]=\sum_{j\in[m]}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\sum_{k\in[n]}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}})({\bm{\mu}}_{k}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*})\right].

so

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}+{\bm{\delta}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}},\bm{\pi})-\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}},\bm{\pi})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\sum_{j\in[m]}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})\sum_{k\in[n]}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})({\bm{\mu}}_{k}+{\bm{\delta}}_{k}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*})\right]

\displaystyle-\sum_{j\in[m]}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}})\sum_{k\in[n]}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}})({\bm{\mu}}_{k}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*})\right]\Bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim p_{*}}\left[\sum_{k\in[n]}\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}}){\bm{\delta}}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\in[m]}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\sum_{k\in[n]}\left(\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})-\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}})\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}})\right)({\bm{\mu}}_{k}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*})\right]\Bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim p_{*}}\left[\sum_{k\in[n]}\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{k}\right\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j\in[m]}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\sum_{k\in[n]}\left|\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})-\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}})\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}})\right|\cdot\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{k}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}\right\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in[n]}\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{k}\right\|_{2}+\sum_{j\in[m]}\pi_{j}^{*}\sum_{k\in[n]}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\left|\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})-\psi_{i}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}})\psi_{k}({\bm{x}}|{\bm{\mu}})\right|\right]\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{k}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in[n]}\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{k}\right\|_{2}+\sum_{j\in[m]}\pi_{j}^{*}\sum_{k\in[n]}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\exp\left(2\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|)\right)-1\right]\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{k}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}\right\|_{2},

where the last inequality is because
\psi_{i},\psi_{k}\leq 1
and applying
\psi_{i}
bound above.

The remaining task is to bound

\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\exp(2\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|))-1\right].

Since

\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{\max\{4D_{\max}+2B,6d\}},

we can use Lemma 18 in
[
42
]
to bound it as

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\exp(2\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|))-1\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp(2\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}\right\|))\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\exp(2\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|\cdot\left\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}\right\|)\right]-1

\displaystyle\leq\exp(2\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(2D_{\max}+B))(1+10\sqrt{d}\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|)-1

\displaystyle=\exp(2\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(2D_{\max}+B))-1+10\sqrt{d}\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|\exp(2\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(2D_{\max}+B))

\displaystyle\leq 4\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(2D_{\max}+B)+10\sqrt{d}\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|\exp(1)\leq(30\sqrt{d}+8D_{\max}+4B)\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|.

where we used
\exp(1+x)\leq 1+2x
,
\forall x\in[0,1]
at the last line. Plugging this back, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}+{\bm{\delta}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})-\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in[n]}\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{k}\right\|+\sum_{k\in[n]}(30\sqrt{d}+8D_{\max}+4B)\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|(2D_{\max}+B)

\displaystyle\lesssim n(\sqrt{d}+D_{\max}+B)(D_{\max}+B)\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|+\sum_{k\in[n]}\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{k}\right\|.

∎

The following lemma is standard in optimization analysis. We show the decreasing of loss given the loss function is local smooth. In particular, as long as the movement
{\bm{\delta}}
is close to gradient direction
-\eta\nabla\mathcal{L}
, we can show the loss decreasing is always on the order of gradient norm
\eta\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}\right\|_{F}^{2}
.

Lemma F.2
.

If
U({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})\leq B^{2}
and movement
\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{\max\{6d,4D_{\max}+2B\}}
, then there is a constant
C
such that

\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)}+{\bm{\delta}},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})-\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})\leq\langle\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)}),{\bm{\delta}}\rangle+C\left(n(\sqrt{d}+D_{\max}+B)(D_{\max}+B)+n\right)\left\|{\bm{\delta}}\right\|_{F}^{2}.

In particular, if
\left\|{\bm{\delta}}+\eta\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}}\mathcal{L}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\eta\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}}\mathcal{L}\right\|_{2}/4
, that is movement
{\bm{\delta}}
is close to the gradient update in Algorithm
1
, and furthermore stepsize
\eta\lesssim\frac{1}{\max\{d,n(\sqrt{d}+D_{\max}+B)(D_{\max}+B)+n\}}
is small enough, then

\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t+1)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})-\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})\leq-\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})\right\|_{F}^{2}.

As a corollary, loss is non-increasing
\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t+1)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})\leq\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)},\bm{\pi}^{(t)})\leq\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(0)},\bm{\pi}^{(0)})
.

Proof.

According to the Algorithm
1
, we focus on the case that weights
\bm{\pi}
is fixed at
\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)}
. Thus, in the following we will omit the dependency on
\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)}
for simplicity.

We have (omit superscript
(t)
for simplicity)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})-\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}\left({\bm{\mu}}+s{\bm{\delta}}\right),{\bm{\delta}}\right\rangle\,{\textnormal{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}),{\bm{\delta}}\rangle\,{\textnormal{d}}s+\int_{0}^{1}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}+s{\bm{\delta}})-\nabla\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}),{\bm{\delta}}\right\rangle\,{\textnormal{d}}s

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}),{\bm{\delta}}\rangle+\int_{0}^{1}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}+s{\bm{\delta}})-\nabla\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}),{\bm{\delta}}\right\rangle\,{\textnormal{d}}s.

Note for
\forall s\in[0,1]
, using Theorem
F.1
we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}+s{\bm{\delta}})-\nabla\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}),{\bm{\delta}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[n]}\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}+{\bm{\delta}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}+s{\bm{\delta}})-\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}})\right\|_{2}\cdot\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i\in[n]}\left(n(\sqrt{d}+D_{\max}+B)(D_{\max}+B)\left\|s{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|_{2}+\sum_{k\in[n]}\left\|s{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(n(\sqrt{d}+D_{\max}+B)(D_{\max}+B)+n\right)\left\|{\bm{\delta}}\right\|_{F}^{2}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}+{\bm{\delta}})-\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}})\leq\langle\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}),{\bm{\delta}}\rangle+C\left(n(\sqrt{d}+D_{\max}+B)(D_{\max}+B)+n\right)\left\|{\bm{\delta}}\right\|_{F}^{2},

with some constant
C
.

When
\left\|{\bm{\delta}}+\eta\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}}\mathcal{L}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\eta\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}}\mathcal{L}\right\|_{2}/4
,
note that the gradient norm can be upper bounded as

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})\right\|_{2}=

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{j}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\sum_{k\in[n]}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}})({\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{(t)}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*})\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{j}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})\sum_{k\in S_{\ell}}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}})({\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{(t)}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*})\right]\right\|_{2}+D_{\max}\leq\sqrt{n}U({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})+D_{\max}\leq\sqrt{n}B+D_{\max}.

Then we have
\left\|{\bm{\delta}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq\tfrac{5}{4}\left\|\eta\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})\right\|_{2}\leq\tfrac{5}{4}\sqrt{n}\eta(\sqrt{n}B+D_{\max})\leq\frac{1}{\max\{6d,4D_{\max}+2B\}}
with small enough
\eta
.

Plugging above into the beginning, since
\eta\lesssim\frac{1}{n(\sqrt{d}+D_{\max}+B)(D_{\max}+B)+n}
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t+1)})-\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{3\eta}{4}\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})\right\|_{F}^{2}+\eta^{2}\mathcal{O}\left(n(\sqrt{d}+D_{\max}+B)(D_{\max}+B)+n\right)\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})\right\|_{F}^{2}.

∎

Appendix G
Proof of main result Theorem
3.1

We give the proof of main result below, which is a combination of global convergence, local convergence, and random initialization.
See
3.1

Proof.

Given random initialization in Lemma
E.1
, we know all conditions for global phase convergence (Theorem
4.2
) hold. Then we can use local convergence (Theorem
4.7
) to get the result.

The parameter recovery results follow from identifiability result Theorem
B.1
.
∎

Appendix H
Sample complexity

In this section, we present the sample complexity results (Theorem
3.3
). Our approach is to show that, with a sufficient polynomial number of samples, the empirical gradient closely approximates the population gradient. This allows us to apply the previously established gradient norm lower bound results (Theorem
C.1
and Theorem
E.3
) to demonstrate convergence.

Specifically, consider the empirical loss
\hat{\mathcal{L}}
defined as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\log\left(\frac{p_{*}({\bm{x}}_{i})}{p({\bm{x}}_{i})}\right)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\ell(p({\bm{x}}_{i}))-\ell(p_{*}({\bm{x}}_{i})),

(32)

where
\ell(u)=-\log u
, consistent with the population loss
\mathcal{L}
in (
4
).

The gradients of the empirical loss are given by

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}_{i})),\quad\nabla_{\bm{\pi}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla_{\bm{\pi}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}_{i})),

(33)

where
\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}_{i}))
and
\nabla_{\bm{\pi}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}_{i}))
are the per-sample gradients.

It is easy to see that these are unbiased estimators as
N\to\infty
. That is,
\mathbf{E}[\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})]=\mathcal{L}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})
,
\mathbf{E}[\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})]=\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})
and
\mathbf{E}[\nabla_{\bm{\pi}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})]=\nabla_{\bm{\pi}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})
.

The algorithm we consider is given in Algorithm
2
. It is identical to Algorithm
1
, except that at each update step, we use
N
fresh samples to compute the empirical loss instead of using the population loss.

Algorithm 2

Online Gradient-EM with near-optimal weight updates

1:

Input:
Stepsize
\eta
,
iterations
T
, target accuracy
{\varepsilon}
, Batch size
N

2:

(Random) Initialization:

\forall i\in[n],{\bm{\mu}}_{i}^{(0)}\sim p^{*},\pi_{i}^{(0)}=\frac{1}{n}
.

3:

for

t=0
to
T

do

4:

Sample
N
fresh data
{\bm{x}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{x}}_{N}\sim p_{*}
and obtain
\hat{\mathcal{L}}
as (
32
).

5:

\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)}\leftarrow\text{$poly({\varepsilon})$-optimal solution of convex subproblem}\arg\min_{\bm{\pi}\in{\mathcal{P}}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})

6:

{\bm{\mu}}^{(t+1)}\leftarrow{\bm{\mu}}^{(t)}-\eta\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)},{\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})

7:

end

for

8:

Output:

{\bm{\mu}}^{(T)},\bm{\pi}^{(T)}

The main result of this section is as follows: it shows that a polynomial number of samples is sufficient to achieve population loss within
{\varepsilon}
and to recover the model parameters.
See
3.3

The key technical components are the following two lemmas, which show that with a polynomial number of samples, the empirical gradients are close to their population counterparts:

Lemma H.1
.

Under Assumption
1
,
2
,
3
, consider any fixed
{\bm{\mu}},\bm{\pi}
such that
U({\bm{\mu}})=\sum_{\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq B
, then for any
\gamma>0
, with
N=O(\frac{B+nD_{\max}^{2}+nd}{\gamma^{2}\delta})
fresh samples, we have with probability at least
1-\delta

\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})-\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})\right\|_{F}\leq\gamma,

where
\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}
is the empirical gradient with
N
fresh samples as in (
33
).

Lemma H.2
.

Under Assumption
1
,
2
,
3
, consider any fixed
{\bm{\mu}},\bm{\pi}
, then for any
\gamma>0
, with
N=O(\frac{n}{\gamma^{2}\delta})
fresh samples, we have with probability at least
1-\delta
that for all
i

\pi_{i}|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})-\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})|\leq\gamma,

where
\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}
is the empirical gradient with
N
fresh samples as in (
33
).

With these concentration results in hand, we can combine them with the convergence results from Appendix
C
and Appendix
E
to derive the following results.

Theorem H.3

(Global convergence)
.

Under the same setting as Theorem
4.2
and suppose batch size
N=O(\frac{nB(D_{\max}^{4}+B^{8})}{\eta{\varepsilon}_{0}^{5}\delta})
is large enough, then there exists threshold
{\varepsilon}_{0}=\exp(-\Theta(\Delta^{2}))
such that with probability
1-\delta
population loss
\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(T_{1})},\bm{\pi}^{(T_{1})})\leq{\varepsilon}_{0}
within time
T_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(B/(\eta{\varepsilon}_{0})\right).

Theorem H.4

(Local convergence)
.

Under the same setting as Theorem
4.7
and suppose batch size
N=O(\frac{\poly(d,m,n,1/\pi_{\min}^{*},1/\lambda_{\min},D_{\max})}{\eta{\varepsilon}^{2.5}\delta})
is large enough, then we have
with probability
1-\delta
population loss
\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(T_{2})},\bm{\pi}^{(T_{2})})\leq{\varepsilon}
within time
T_{2}-T_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(A/(\eta\sqrt{{\varepsilon}})\right),
where
A=\poly(d,m,n,1/\pi_{\min}^{*},1/\lambda_{\min},D_{\max})
.

Proof of Theorem
3.3
.

The result follows by combining the initialization guarantee from Theorem
E.1
, the global convergence result from Theorem
H.3
, and the local convergence result from Theorem
H.4
.
∎

H.1
Omitted proofs

We present the omitted proofs in this section.

The following lemma shows that a polynomial number of samples is sufficient to ensure that the empirical gradient is close to the population gradient. The proof follows from standard concentration inequalities, leveraging the fact that all quantities are bounded.
See
H.1

Proof.

For any
\gamma>0
. by Markov inequality, we know

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}-\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}\right\|_{F}\geq\gamma\right)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{x}}_{N}}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}-\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}N}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}))-\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}N}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}))\right\|_{F}^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}N}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}N}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}))\right\|_{F}^{2}\right],

where in the last line we use Jensen’s inequality and
\mathbf{E}_{\bm{x}}[\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}))]=\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}
.

Note that for any
{\bm{\mu}}_{i}

\displaystyle\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}))=\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})({\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{x}}),

so we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}}\left[\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}))\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}}\left[\left\|\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})({\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{x}})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]=\sum_{j}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\left\|\psi_{i}({\bm{x}})({\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{x}})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\pi_{j}^{*}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim j}\left[\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{j}^{*}-{\bm{x}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}+D_{\max}^{2}+d.

Thus, plugging back we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}-\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}\right\|_{F}\geq\gamma\right)\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{B+nD_{\max}^{2}+nd}{\gamma^{2}N}

∎

Similarly, the following lemma shows updating weights
\bm{\pi}
on polynomial samples is enough to make sure it is close to its population update counterpart.
See
H.2

Proof.

We have by Markov inequality that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\pi_{i}|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})-\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})|\geq\gamma\right)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{x}}_{N}}\left[|\pi_{i}\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})-\pi_{i}\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}N}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}}\left[|\pi_{i}\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}))-\pi_{i}\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}N}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}}\left[|\pi_{i}\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}))|^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}N}|\pi_{i}\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{\pi},{\bm{\mu}})|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}N}\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}}\left[|\pi_{i}\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}))|^{2}\right].

Note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}}\left[|\pi_{i}\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\ell(p({\bm{x}}))|^{2}\right]=\mathbf{E}_{{\bm{x}}}\left[\left|\frac{\pi_{i}\phi({\bm{\mu}}_{i};{\bm{x}})}{p({\bm{x}})}\right|^{2}\right]\leq 1.

Plugging in back gives the desired bound.
∎

The following result is a finite-sample version of the global convergence guarantee in Theorem
4.2
. The key idea in the proof is to use the concentration results above to show that the empirical gradients remain close to the population gradients, allowing us to reuse the earlier convergence analysis.
See
H.3

Proof.

By Lemma
H.2
and Lemma
H.1
we know that with probability at least
1-\delta/T_{1}

\displaystyle\pi_{i}|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}-\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}|\leq\gamma\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{0}^{2}/(D_{\max}^{2}+B^{4}),\quad\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}-\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}\right\|_{F}\leq\gamma\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{0}^{2}/B\leq\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}\right\|_{F}/4

given
N=O(\frac{n(D_{\max}^{4}+B^{8})}{{\varepsilon}_{0}^{4}\delta}T_{1})
samples. Using union bound, we know above bounds hold for all
t\leq T_{1}
with probability
1-\delta
.

First by Algorithm
2
we know
\pi_{i}|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}+1|\leq{\varepsilon}_{\pi}
. Together with
\pi_{i}|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}-\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}|\leq\gamma\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{0}^{2}/(D_{\max}^{2}+B^{4})
, we know
\pi_{i}|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}+1|\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{0}^{2}/(D_{\max}^{2}+B^{4})
that satisfies the condition of Theorem
E.3
. Moreover, by Lemma
E.2
we know

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\in[m]}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*},\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}\rangle\geq\sum_{\ell\in[m]}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*},\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}\rangle-B\gamma\gtrsim\min\left\{\frac{1}{d},\mathcal{L}^{2}\right\}.

Then, follow the same argument in the proof of Theorem
E.4
we know
U({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})\leq B^{2}
for all
t\leq T_{1}.
Thus, we can use Theorem
E.3
now.

Now using Lemma
F.2
we know

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t+1)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})-\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})\leq-\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})\right\|_{F}^{2}\lesssim-\frac{\eta}{B}\min\left\{\frac{1}{d},\mathcal{L}^{2}\right\}.

Then follow the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
4.2
we know the convergence time
T_{1}=O(B/(\eta{\varepsilon}_{0}))
.
∎

Similarly, we obtain a local convergence result using only a polynomial number of samples. This serves as the finite-sample counterpart to Theorem
4.7
.
See
H.4

Proof.

By Lemma
H.2
and Lemma
H.1
we know that with probability at least
1-\delta/(T-T_{1})

\displaystyle\pi_{i}|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}-\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}|\leq\gamma\lesssim{\varepsilon}/n,\quad\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}-\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}\right\|_{F}\leq\gamma\lesssim{\varepsilon}/B\leq\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}\right\|_{F}/4

given
N=O(\frac{nB^{2}D_{\max}^{2}}{{\varepsilon}^{2}\delta}(T_{2}-T_{1}))
samples, and
A=\poly(d,m,n,1/\pi_{\min}^{*},1/\lambda_{\min},D_{\max})
as in Theorem
C.1
. Using union bound, we know above bounds hold for all
T_{1}\leq t\leq T_{2}
with probability
1-\delta
.

First by Algorithm
2
we know
\pi_{i}|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}+1|\leq{\varepsilon}_{\pi}
. Together with
\pi_{i}|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}-\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}|\leq\gamma\lesssim{\varepsilon}/n
, we know
\pi_{i}|\nabla_{\pi_{i}}\mathcal{L}+1|\lesssim{\varepsilon}/n
that satisfies the condition of Theorem
C.1
. Moreover, by Lemma
C.4
we know

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\in[m]}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*},\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\hat{\mathcal{L}}\rangle\geq\sum_{\ell\in[m]}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{i}-{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*},\nabla_{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}\rangle-B\gamma\geq\mathcal{L}/8.

Then, follow the same argument in the proof of Theorem
C.2
we know
U({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)})\leq B^{2}
for all
T_{1}\leq t\leq T_{2}.
Thus, we can use Theorem
C.1
now.

Now using Lemma
F.2
we know

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t+1)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})-\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})\leq-\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\mu}}\mathcal{L}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)})\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq-\frac{\eta}{A}\mathcal{L}^{3/2}({\bm{\mu}}^{(t)},\bm{\pi}^{(t+1)}).

Then follow the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
4.2
we know the convergence time
T_{2}-T_{1}=O(A/(\eta\sqrt{{\varepsilon}}))
.
∎
